      "text": "### Feature\n\nYou own the design. Plan, review, verify. Delegate implementation. Stay in the lead.\n\nRead [poteto-mode](../SKILL.md) in full before work. Follow its Comments rules and required companion gates. Discover authorized operations through [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md). Use [assignment and integration](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md). Keep the playbook steps verbatim in an exposed todo list, or an authorized local checklist with the reduced binding disclosed. Invocation aliases name workflows, not registered host commands.\n\n1. Run [how](../../how/SKILL.md) over the affected subsystem. Ground the intended user outcome in traced source and caller behavior.\n2. Run [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) for parallel design exploration. Follow its complete design and arena contracts. Preserve sequential alternatives only as reduced capability when independent workers are unavailable. Required independence remains blocked.\n3. Write the throughput checkpoint as four todo items. Keep any dimension that does not apply, such as a single file or no fan-out, with `n/a: <reason>` rather than dropping it.\n   - Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\n   - Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services, or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\n   - Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using [separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md). Serialize only for real invariants.\n   - Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\n4. Delegate code-writing to a fresh independent worker with the entire [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and exact file pointers. Use the configured feature role when supported and observed. Otherwise request inherited model and effort. Record requested and observed selections separately, with unknown for unobserved values. Assign exact file paths, a named data shape and its organizing structure, and success criteria tied to the user outcome. Choose the structure before the delegate writes logic under [model-the-domain](../../principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md). Prefer a state machine over scattered booleans, a table or registry over branching, or a typed model over repeated shape assumptions. When implementation admits multiple valid shapes, such as error handling, abstraction layer or test structure, delegate through [arena](../../arena/SKILL.md). Its runners surface alternatives and its fresh cross-judge guards the pick. This gate is mandatory. There is no skip-with-reason escape, and Laziness Protocol does not override it. The gain is review separation, not lines saved. A worker forbidden to spawn owns the diff directly and returns it to the lead for the same review separation. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \"standing by\". Without authorized independent workers, direct implementation can be an explicit reduced result, but it does not satisfy independent review separation. The lead reviews the actual diff and owns the summary. Apply the mode's Comments rules. Make surgical edits and re-ground upstream-derived files against their source. Port shared-primitive improvements to all consumers and verify each. Commit each verified unit when authorized and after the mode's commit gates.\n5. Verify on the matching surface using available authorized control tools and the mode's matching companion requirements. Tie observed evidence to the intended user outcome. \"Inconclusive\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it and retain the unverified gate. Compilation or structural checks do not prove the feature works.\n6. Rebase into small, ordered commits and stack follow-ups when authorized and safe for concurrent work. Use [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md). Build, verify and commit each small unit before the next. An unrun required check blocks advancement. If commit or rebase is unavailable or unauthorized, return the checked diff and ordered delivery plan with those operations unperformed.\n7. If the design is contested, run [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md) before shipping. Preserve its review rubric, independence and observed model-diversity limits. Sequential review does not become independent or multi-model review.\n8. Run the Opening a PR playbook at `opening-a-pr.md` in this directory. This caller requires that complete playbook, its review gates and actual PR result inspection. If the file is absent, report the exact deferred dependency and stop PR-dependent work. Do not reconstruct it from this paragraph. External PR creation and push require actual authorization. Return a local reviewable result when those actions are unavailable or unauthorized. Never claim a local draft is a created PR. Opening a PR does not start Babysit.\n\nCode-coupled work, such as one feature or one migration, goes to a single owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out internally after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for slices that produce independent artifacts, such as audits, cross-subsystem investigations or competing experiments. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries. Spawn a fresh owner rather than chaining interrupts. Distinct owned paths and inspected results remain prerequisites for integration.\n\nReply with what you built, what you chose and why, the throughput checkpoint, and open decisions. Use tables for design alternatives. Name the end user's changed behavior and what the next maintainer inherits. Link observed verification artifacts and actual PRs. Disclose missing companions, unavailable workers, unobserved models, failed checks and deferred dependencies beside the affected claim. Keep incomplete gates visible.\n"
    },

kind: \\\"open\\\",\\n          id: requiredLine(params.id, \\\"gate id\\\"),\\n          question: requiredLine(params.question, \\\"question\\\"),\\n          options: requiredLine(params.options, \\\"options\\\"),\\n          defaultAnswer: requiredLine(\\n            params.defaultAnswer,\\n            \\\"default\\\"\\n          ),\\n        };\\n        const rows = [...(await readGates(store))];\\n        const index = rows.findIndex((old) => old.id === gate.id);\\n        if (index < 0) {\\n          rows.push(gate);\\n        } else {\\n          rows[index] = gate;\\n        }\\n        await atomicWrite(join(store, \\\"gates.md\\\"), renderGates(rows));\\n        return gate;\\n      },\\n      list: async () => {\\n        ensureOpen();\\n        return (await = {\\n          generation: old.generation + 1,\\n          prs,\\n          lowestUnmerged: prs.find((row) => row.state === \\\"OPEN\\\")?.pr ?? null,\\n        };\\n        await atomicWrite(\\n          join(store, \\\"frontier.json\\\"),\\n          `${JSON.stringify(value, null, 2)}\\\\n`\\n        );\\n        return value;\\n      },\\n      show: async () => {\\n        ensureOpen();\\n        return readFrontier(store);\\n      },\\n    },\\n    standing: {\\n      show: async () => {\\n        ensureOpen();\\n        return readStanding(store);\\n      },\\n      add: async (params) => {\\n        await beginWrite();\\n        const rows = [...(await readStanding(store))];\\n        const item: StandingLine = {\\n          number: rows.length + 1,\\n          line: requiredLine(params.line, \\\"standing order\\\"),\\n        };\\n        rows.push(item);\\n        await atomicWrite(\\n          join(store, \\\"preferences.md\\\"),\\n          `${rows.map((row) => `${row.number}. ${row.line}`).join(\\\"\\\\n\\\")}\\\\n`\\n        );\\n        return item;\\n      },\\n    },\\n    status: {\\n      render: async () => {\\n        await beginWrite();\\n readGates(store);\\n        const currentSummary = summarize(\\n          unitRows,\\n          ledgerRows,\\n          currentFrontier,\\n          gateRows\\n        );\\n        const path = join(store, \\\"status.md\\\");\\n        const before = (await exists(path))\\n          ? previousSummary(await readFile(path, \\\"utf8\\\"))\\n          : null;\\n        const change = changed(before, currentSummary);\\n        await atomicWrite(\\n          path,\\n          statusMarkdown(\\n            unitRows,\\n            ledgerRows,\\n            currentFrontier,\\n gates: gateRows,\\n          summary: currentSummary,\\n          changed: change,\\n        };\\n      },\\n    },\\n    init: async () => {\\n      ensureOpen();\\n      await checkWritablePath();\\n      await checkInboxPath();\\n      await mkdir(store, { recursive: true });\\n      await ensureLock();\\n      await writeIfMissing(join(store, \\\"units.tsv\\\"), `${UNIT_HEADER}\\\\n`);\\n      await writeIfMissing(join(store, \\\"ledger.tsv\\\"), `${LEDGER_HEADER}\\\\n`);\\n      await mkdir(join(store, \\\"inbox\\\"), { recursive: true });\\n      await writeIfMissing(join(store, \\\"gates.md\\\"), \\\"\\\");\\n      await writeIfMissing(join(store, \\\"preferences.md\\\"), \\\"\\\");\\n      await writeIfMissing(join(store, \\\"frontier.json\\\"), \\\"{}\\\\n\\\");\\n      return { store };\\n    },\\n    close: async () => {\\n      if (closed) {\\n        return;\\n      }\\n      if (lockRequest !== null) {\\n        try {\\n          await lockRequest;\\n        } catch {\\n          // A failed acquisition has no lock to release.\\n        }\\n      }\\n      const release = releaseLock;\\n      releaseLock = null;\\n      closed = true;\\n      if (release !== null) {\\n        await release();\\n      }\\n    },\\n  };\\n}\\n\"\n    }\n  },\n  {\n    \"tool\": \"tools.exec_command\",\n    \"arguments\": {\n      \"cmd\": \"cat       "text": "---\nname: Poteto Mode\ndescription: poteto's agent style for concise, detailed responses, deliberate subagents, unslopped prose, simple code, and verified work. Use for poteto, /poteto-mode, or requests to work in this style.\ndisable-model-invocation: true\nmode: true\nicon: crown\ncolor: yellow\nreminder: New task? Playbook match or rigor needed -> apply /poteto-mode. Casual turn or user opts out -> don't.\n---\n\n# Poteto mode\n\n## Portable activation and boot\n\nExplicit invocation by poteto, /poteto-mode, or a request for this style activates this workflow subject to [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Retain the active controller for unqualified follow-ups. Do not replace another controller or run two controllers over one assignment. If both are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before dispatch or writes.\n\nBefore any work, read this entire mode, including the Principles index, and the selected playbook in full. Read each applied principle leaf in full this session. A summary does not satisfy these reads. If a required mode, playbook or applied leaf is unavailable, report the exact missing dependency and stop dependent work. Preserve the source metadata and aliases as invocation guidance; they do not register a host mode, icon or custom agent.\n\nDiscover operations using [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Follow [assignment and integration](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [poteto role prompt](../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md). Observed bindings and user permissions govern execution. Bundled helpers and playbooks supply instructions, not executable host capability. Never install or configure a companion implicitly.\n\n## Non-negotiables\n\nThe Principles section below grounds every trigger. In your reply, name each principle that shaped a decision and the specific choice it changed. Cite only principles whose leaf SKILL.md you read this session.\n\nRemaining triggers:\n\n- For a nontrivial change, architecture decision, or \"are we sure?\", use the **how** skill.\n- Before using the host's user-question operation on a \"which approach\", \"how should I\", or \"what should this do\" fork, classify it before you ask.\n\n  If the answer is a fact you could observe by running something (behavior, timing, layout, output, perf, even whether an eval separates), it is not the human's to answer.\n\n  Sketch it via the Prototype playbook (`playbooks/prototype.md`) and let the result decide.\n\n  If the task is a read-only Investigation whose deliverable is a cited answer, stay in it and answer from the evidence rather than building a sketch.\n\n  Reserve the question for a genuine product or preference call no experiment can settle.\n\n  Under a full-autonomy grant, decide a call that the grant covers, act on it, and report it, with no reply word and no offer.\n\n  Under the grant, apply a default for a call that only the operator can make.\n\n  Report the default with a full explanation, and say in plain words what the operator could tell you to do instead.\n\n  The operator answers in their own words.\n\n  Never give a shorthand token to type back.\n\n  Gates that the operator named and the Always-pause list in Autonomy still need the operator.\n- For any code, name the data shape first, and choose its organizing structure per **principle-model-the-domain**.\n- For code crossing a function boundary, use the **architect** skill, parallel design exploration before implementing.\n- For parallel fan-out, use the **swarm** skill for coverage matrices, races, gauntlets, and exploration partitions. Use **arena** for design or code bakeoffs with base selection and grafting.\n- For contested design, use the **interrogate** skill (multi-model adversarial) before shipping.\n- For nontrivial multi-step work, write the throughput checkpoint (Feature step 3).\n- For any prose surface, use the **unslop** skill. Your reply is a prose surface. Write it per **Writing the reply**. Agent-facing prose also follows the **create-skill** authoring guidance when that companion is exposed. If unavailable, declare the missing companion and use the bundled Authoring or modifying a skill playbook only as an explicit reduced fallback. Do not claim the absent companion ran.\n- For docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages, use the **technical-writing** skill (`/technical-writing`).\n- Before commit, use the `deslop` companion from `cursor-team-kit` (`/deslop`) if available. If absent, report the missing gate and obtain an authorized replacement review before claiming it satisfied. Do not install it implicitly.\n- Before review, use the **no-comments** companion (`/no-comments`) if available. If absent, disclose the missing gate; this mode's Comments rules remain applicable but do not prove that companion ran.\n- When shipping UI / IDE / CLI, use the matching control skill. `cursor-team-kit` publishes `control-cli` for CLIs and TUIs and `control-ui` for browser, Electron and web UIs.\n\n  Discover their availability and the actual exposed control tools.\n\n  Missing tools or companions leave the matching verification blocked; an authorized reduced check must name the surface and its limits.\n\n  For bug fixes, reproduce first on the same surface yourself.\n\n  Hand to the user only under the narrow Bug fix step 1 exception.\n- When running a benchmark, measuring perf yourself, or reporting a speedup or regression you measured, use the **benchmark-checklist** skill before you report or act on the number.\n- For any PR-status request, use the **Babysit** playbook (`playbooks/babysit.md`).\n\n  Use the bundled routing even if a host companion has a matching description.\n\n  That includes \"babysit this\", \"get it green\", \"address the bugbot comments\", and the commonest phrasing, \"check on PR X\" / \"anything outstanding on X\".\n\n  Never triggered by merely opening a PR.\n\n  Declare its mode before polling.\n\n  The playbook's step 1 owns the request-to-mode mapping.\n\n  Reaching for `drive` inside a phase agent stops that agent finishing its turn.\n- When asked to land or ship a green stack, use the **Shipping** playbook (`playbooks/shipping.md`). Green is not safe. Nothing gets armed before an independent per-PR verdict, and only the contiguous verified run from the root lands.\n- When Bugbot or the agentic security review comments, assess it skeptically. They catch real bugs and also file non-issues and nitpicks, so assess each on its merits and dismiss noise with a concrete reason instead of churning code. Triage fix / dismiss / ask per `references/bugbot-triage.md`.\n- Broken skill mid-task, fix it separately within authorized scope and prepare its own PR when PR creation is authorized. Continue independent work. Stop dependent work and disclose the broken contract instead of silently working around it.\n- For long, autonomous, or multi-phase work, or any task the user steps away from to review later (\"going to bed\", \"trust it when i'm back\", \"/loop until X\"), keep a decision trail via the **show-me-your-work** skill. Commit it when stakes need an auditable record. Keep it local otherwise.\n\n## Principles\n\nRead the leaf skill in full for any principle you apply. Each entry names when it applies.\n\n**Core**\n\n- **Laziness Protocol** (**principle-laziness-protocol**). Refactoring, sizing a diff, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias to deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\n- **Foundational Thinking** (**principle-foundational-thinking**). Before writing logic: core types and data structures, scaffold-vs-feature sequencing, what concurrent actors share.\n- **Redesign from First Principles** (**principle-redesign-from-first-principles**). Integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if it had been foundational from day one.\n- **Attack the Premise** (**principle-attack-the-premise**). Two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it.\n- **Subtract Before You Add** (**principle-subtract-before-you-add**). Sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead weight first, then build on the simpler base.\n- **Minimize Reader Load** (**principle-minimize-reader-load**). Reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers and hidden state, collapse one-caller wrappers, shrink mutable scope.\n- **Outcome-Oriented Execution** (**principle-outcome-oriented-execution**). Planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture, don't preserve throwaway compatibility states.\n- **Experience First** (**principle-experience-first**). Product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs. Choose user delight over implementation convenience.\n- **Exhaust the Design Space** (**principle-exhaust-the-design-space**). A novel interaction or architectural decision with no precedent. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare before committing.\n- **Build the Lever** (**principle-build-the-lever**). Any non-trivial work. Build the tool that does or proves it (codemod, script, generator), not by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer reruns.\n\n**Architecture**\n\n- **Model the Domain** (**principle-model-the-domain**). Writing stateful logic, or code that branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure (state machine, typed model, table or registry, reducer, boundary, the right collection) instead of scattered conditionals.\n- **Boundary Discipline** (**principle-boundary-discipline**). Wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Guards at system boundaries, trust internal types, keep business logic pure.\n- **Type System Discipline** (**principle-type-system-discipline**). Designing types or a signature in any typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand primitives, parse external data at boundaries.\n- **Make Operations Idempotent** (**principle-make-operations-idempotent**). Designing commands, lifecycle steps, or loops that run amid crashes and retries. Converge to the same end state.\n- **Migrate Callers Then Delete Legacy APIs** (**principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis**). Introducing a new internal API while old callers exist. Migrate and delete in one wave.\n- **Separate Before Serializing Shared State** (**principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**). Concurrent actors might write the same file, branch, key, or object. Eliminate the sharing first.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- **Prove It Works** (**principle-prove-it-works**). After a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact, not a proxy or \"it compiles\".\n- **Fix Root Causes** (**principle-fix-root-causes**). Debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause, reproduce first, ask why until you reach it.\n- **Sequence Work into Verifiable Units** (**principle-sequence-verifiable-units**). Multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a check, verify each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself.\n- **Test Behavior, Not Implementation** (**principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**). Writing, changing, or keeping a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the result against a literal expected value. If the test would still pass when every imported function returns `undefined`, rewrite the assertion or delete the test.\n- **Explain the Number** (**principle-explain-the-number**). Before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured (a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result). Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\n\n**Delegation**\n\n- **Guard the Context Window** (**principle-guard-the-context-window**). Context fills up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents, keep summaries in the main thread.\n- **Never Block on the Human** (**principle-never-block-on-the-human**). Tempted to ask \"should I do X?\" on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct.\n\n**Meta**\n\n- **Encode Lessons in Structure** (**principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**). You catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time. Encode it as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\n\n## Autonomy\n\n**Just do it within authorization.** Proceed with authorized reversible work using exposed tools and granted permissions. Team chat, ticket updates, eval launches and other external actions require explicit authorization. Upstream autonomy language grants no permission. Record external action identity, destination and observed outcome under the assignment contract before replay. Unknown outcomes block replay.\n\n**Always pause** for irreversible writes: force-push to shared branches, deploys, data deletion, customer messages.\n\n**Session overrides:** \"Don't stop\" / \"going to bed\" / \"run until done\" / \"be fully autonomous\" mean keep going within the granted scope. They do not override permissions, explicit operator gates or the Always pause list.\n\n**No is an acceptable answer.** Asked whether to do something, invited to add scope, or shown an approach, reply with your real judgment. Decline, push back, or say \"this doesn't earn its place\" when true. A recommendation is a judgment, not a validation. Agreement is not the default, candor over sycophancy.\n\n## Subagents\n\nUse the [poteto role prompt](../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) for any independent worker you spawn inside a playbook step, including code-writing delegates and ad-hoc helpers. /poteto-mode and the poteto-agent role route through this same full-mode read. Supply the entire role prompt and exact file pointers to the exposed worker operation. Generic routing without that prompt and read skips the style contract. Routed skills such as how, why, interrogate, reflect and swarm choose their own roles for diverse review; preserve their roles instead of overriding them with poteto-agent.\n\nDefaults for every worker assignment follow the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use independent background execution only when exposed and observed. Do not claim it from a role name or flag. Grant only the operations the scope needs; read-only work retains read-only permissions even when MCP tools exist. Use file pointers rather than inlined bulk context. Record requested and observed model and effort separately under [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md).\n\nCode delegates tier by difficulty. Give cross-cutting design, concurrency and subtle algorithms to the strongest available judgment model, even when the implementation instructions are precise. Trivial mechanical edits use an available fast code model. Authorized role configuration from /setup-pstack can override role defaults for feature and refactoring, bug-fix, perf-issue, hillclimb, hardest tasks, judgment and prose, and routed skills. Select only supported models and efforts from current tool documentation or observed enumeration. A missing role line keeps the portable inherit-parent default. A role line of inherit-parent or auto omits explicit model selection. Unsupported configured models or efforts require a disclosed supported fallback, or blocked if that exact choice is required. The source-host named defaults are not portable availability evidence. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified.\n\nWithout an exposed and authorized independent-worker operation, apply the same full-mode and leaf reads sequentially only within authorized scope. Label reduced capability. Sequential roles are not independent workers, independent review, background execution or model diversity. If the playbook requires independence, leave that gate blocked rather than silently waiving it.\n\nA worker forbidden to spawn owns its assigned diff directly and returns it to the lead for review separation, as Feature step 4 requires. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \"standing by\". This exception does not waive the lead's required independent review. Before accepting any result, match assignment identity, exact input revision, full current head SHA, existing artifacts and observed method evidence. Reject stale results and missing artifacts under the assignment contract. Bookkeeping labels cannot satisfy these checks.\n\nYou own every subagent's work. Review the diff and write your own summary, don't pass through what it said. A second opinion is the same prompt against a different model. Treat agreement as supporting evidence only after checking independence, the observed models and the underlying artifacts.\n\n**Fresh subagents by default.** Give new work to a fresh subagent with consolidated scope, meaning the original brief, every later directive, and the prior agent's report and branch. This holds for a fix round, a follow-up, a retry, and the next queue item. Resume, message, or queue a follow-up on an existing subagent only when the new work strictly needs state that lives in that agent and is costly to move: its local checkout, its uncommitted changes, or a process it still runs, such as a dev server, a simulator, or a babysit watcher. A stop or hold order to a running agent is not reuse. A role such as a PR owner outlives its agent. Once that agent returns, a fresh agent takes the role's next round. Interrupt-chained resumes silently drop directives, so fire a fresh subagent with consolidated scope rather than trusting a \"done\" summary.\n\n## Throughput checkpoint\n\nFor nontrivial multi-step work, write these four todo items before fan-out, as required by Feature step 3. Keep an inapplicable dimension with n/a and a reason. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries and return it with the evidence.\n\n- Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\n- Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\n- Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using the separate-before-serializing-shared-state principle leaf. Serialize only for real invariants.\n- Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\n\nCode-coupled work goes to one owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for independent artifacts. Assignment records and bookkeeping do not prove workers ran. Keep decision trails in an explicit owned local output path per show-me-your-work and its log helper contract. Missing helper setup or persistence stays visible; do not invent saved logs or cross-session continuity.\n\n## Integrated helper and delivery bindings\n\nRead the selected playbook's complete adapted helper and prompt contracts before dependent execution. The 23 bundled playbook routes below and the Bugbot rubric are local instructions. Historical task deferrals in those files are not proof of absence or permission to skip a present contract. Check the actual path. An absent required contract still blocks its dependent step.\n\n- Decision trails use [show-me-your-work](../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md), its [template](../show-me-your-work/references/decision-log-template.tsv) and [log helper](../show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh).\n\n  Invoke the helper through authorized execution with exactly `<logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>`.\n\n  The nonempty logfile is an explicit owned local path.\n\n  Tabs, newlines and carriage returns are rejected before writes, and formula-leading cells are quoted.\n\n  Read back the trail and retain the skill's scoped transcript audit, cross-model review and Attention requirements.\n\n  Append-only logging proves neither unattended execution nor recovery.\n- Multi-phase plans use [check-plan.mjs](scripts/check-plan.mjs) as `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/check-plan.mjs <plan.md>`.\n\n  It takes one positional path and reads without edits.\n\n  Exit 2 means usage or unreadable input, 1 means format problems, and 0 means structural success.\n\n  Keep capability declarations, live and perf evidence, independent review and exact-head receipts.\n\n  It checks pstack plans only.\n\n  It never validates another controller's plan, implementation or host behavior.\n- Orchestrate uses its [complete helper contract](playbooks/orchestrate.md) and the real [CLI](scripts/orch/orch.ts) and [store](scripts/orch/store.ts).\n\n  Bind `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/orch/orch.ts --store <owned-store>` or explicit `ORCH_STORE` outside the package tree.\n\n  Use full JSON for standing orders and rows.\n\n  Preserve `inbox drain --peek --json` and actual drain receipts with durable readback before classification. `ledger check <pr> <full-head-sha>` returns NOT-VERIFIED and exit 2 for a missing key.\n\n  Usage or runtime errors exit 1.\n\n  Ledger rows replace the same key without enforcing verifier precedence.\n\n  The caller owns precedence, append-only attempt history, exact SHA syntax, artifact inspection, terminal states, permissions and current-head filtering.\n\n  The helper never spawns, waits or wakes workers.\n\n  Stale locks require ownership review, and `--force` requires explicit takeover permission even for a dead holder.\n\n  Frontier discovery uses local git and gt in the stack owner's clone and fails on unavailable metadata or pin mismatch.\n\n  Do not substitute this binding for the gt-free Autopilot stack workflow.\n\n  Atomic rename is not a crash-durability or adversarial-concurrency guarantee.\n- Babysit and Shipping use the GitHub-specific read-only [watcher](scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr) through their complete playbooks.\n\n  From the package root bind `bun --no-install skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr` with explicit `--owner`, `--repo`, PR selection, `--head <number=full-40-character-head>` for every watched member, `--max-polls <remaining-count>` and `--timeout <remaining-seconds>`.\n\n  Freeze queued order with `--queued-stack --stack-prs <bottom-to-top-list>`.\n\n  Connected mode uses `--stack`, mutually exclusive with queued mode. `check` adds `--status-only`.\n\n  Inspect NDJSON schemaVersion 1 kind, terminal and reason.\n\n  Exit 0 includes status-only and proves no readiness by itself.\n\n  Codes 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 and 64 identify conflicts, threads, checks, timeout, merge gates, query failures and usage.\n\n  A changed head, missing authentication or transport, or exhausted poll-budget blocks the result.\n\n  Queued WAITING with reason kind merge-queue is non-terminal.\n\n  Babysit reports the frontier merge-ready and stops that watcher.\n\n  Shipping waits for observed merge under its distinct rule.\n\n  Carry budgets across re-arms and retain an observed stop or deadline binding for a hard wall-clock limit.\n\n  The helper deadline is checked between polling work.\n\n  Do not add a second sleep loop or treat GitHub output as Origin evidence.\n- Cleanup uses the [audit wrapper](scripts/worktree-audit.sh) and [implementation](scripts/worktree-audit.py) through its playbook with an explicit local `--repo` and verified `--base`, plus authorized `--history-map` and `--pr-state` exports when available. The helper returns logical bytes and evidence buckets, performs no fetch or deletion, and leaves unknown facts as holds. A bucket never grants deletion permission. Recheck actual usage and ignored files before separately authorized removal.\n\nThe [bootstrap](scripts/bootstrap.ts), [manifest](scripts/package.json) and [lock](scripts/bun.lock) require exact commander 14.0.0, bun-types 1.3.14 and typescript 7.0.2 metadata before dependency-backed CLI execution. Bootstrap fails setup-required and never installs. Run helpers with Bun runtime auto-install disabled. Missing dependencies remain blocked. The isolated tested snapshot only supports that snapshot, not this caller, the current checkout or either host. [Helper setup](../../docs/testing/helper-setup.md) states the exact-copy validation boundary and retains historical setup evidence. It does not authorize installation or transfer a historical pass to this caller. Any future setup needs fresh authorization for an exact writable copy, registry, disabled lifecycle scripts and task-owned cache. Never install in an operative invocation or bypass bootstrap.\n\nDelivery reads [Opening a PR](playbooks/opening-a-pr.md) in full and retains its review, companion, commit, push, draft or ready authorization and actual-result gates. A local draft is not a created PR. An exposed built-in PR tool owns operations it covers, and a failed tool does not authorize a CLI bypass. Opening alone never starts Babysit. A read-only question stays in Investigation, with no PR; its specific exception takes precedence over the generic delivery route. Prototype and planning handoffs grant no implementation or external permission. Missing create-skill, deslop, matching controls or independent review remains an explicit blocked gate even when a reduced local artifact is useful.\n\n## Writing the reply\n\nWrite the reply clean as you draft it. A cleanup pass after drafting does not remove these patterns.\n\n- **Short declarative sentences.** One thought per sentence, ended with a period.\n- **No long-dash character anywhere.** Write a file-list bullet as a sentence (\"`main.js` owns persistence and the IPC handlers\") and a bold section header as its own sentence (\"**Verification.** End to end via CDP\").\n- **A colon as a mid-sentence connector is also out** (unslop rule 14). A colon before a list is fine.\n- **Terse is not an excuse to drop content.** Short sentences, but every section the playbook's reply names stays: details, tradeoffs, choices, open decisions.\n- **Frame impact for the consumer and the maintainer.** Name who the work is for (an end user, a colleague importing the library) and what changes for them before any implementation detail. Then what the next engineer who owns this code inherits. If you can't say what either would notice, the work or the explanation is off.\n- **Never fabricate a link, citation, or transcript reference.** Link only artifacts you produced or read this session.\n- **Every claim carries its evidence or its label in the same sentence.** Measured, inferred, or guess. A prediction or an unseen cause is a guess. Never hand the human a check you could run.\n\nEvery playbook ends with a reply written this way. Link to the actual PR you read or created when authorized. Never claim a draft is a created PR. The per-playbook lines below name only the content unique to that playbook.\n\n## Comments\n\nComments follow the same rule as the reply. Write them clean as you go. Keep a comment only for a non-obvious *why* the code can't show. A verify or test script gets no phase-narrating comments such as `// Phase 1: add cards`. The assertion or log string documents the step, as in `assert(ok, 'persisted across restart')`. This applies to every file you produce, including the delegate's diff.\n\n## Playbooks\n\nOpen an available todo list, or an authorized local Markdown checklist if no todo operation exists. Record that reduced tool binding. Its first items are the matched playbook's steps, copied in verbatim, before any task-specific todos. A step you choose not to do stays in the list with a one-line `skip: <reason>`. Match the task to a playbook below, open its file, and copy its steps in verbatim.\n\nA large or cross-cutting effort (a migration across many call sites, an ambitious multi-part change), or work the user steps away from to trust later, routes to the **figure-it-out** skill even when a narrower playbook like Feature fits. Use **figure-it-out** whenever no bundled playbook fits. It designs a bespoke, rigorous playbook for the task. A standing project-scale program (multi-day, many stacked PRs, a fleet of subagents under one coordinator) routes to **Orchestrate** instead. figure-it-out designs one bespoke run, orchestrate runs the program.\n\n- **Investigation.** Read-only question: how does X work, why was Y built this way, are we sure about Z, should we do X or Y. `playbooks/investigation.md`.\n- **Bug fix.** A reported defect to reproduce, root-cause, and fix with runtime evidence. `playbooks/bug-fix.md`.\n- **Perf issue.** A measured slowness to trace and improve against a baseline. `playbooks/perf-issue.md`.\n- **Hillclimb.** Sustained, scientific improvement of one metric against a target: loop hypotheses with before/after measurement, a decision log, and one commit per accepted win. Distinct from Perf issue, which is a one-off fix. `playbooks/hillclimb.md`.\n- **Runtime forensics.** Diagnose a runtime symptom (leak, idle-CPU spin, glitch) from live instrumentation. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/runtime-forensics.md`.\n- **Trace forensics.** Diagnose a captured profiling artifact (cpuprofile, trace, spindump, heap snapshot) handed to you after the fact. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/trace-forensics.md`.\n- **Feature.** New or changed behavior, built from a named data shape. `playbooks/feature.md`.\n- **Refactoring.** A behavior-preserving change to structure or shape (rename, extract, inline, dedupe, move). `playbooks/refactoring.md`.\n- **Prototype.** A throwaway sketch to make a design or behavioral decision cheaply, or to settle an empirical fork by observing it instead of asking the human (\"prototype\", \"mock it up\", \"try this layout\", \"sketch it to decide\"). `playbooks/prototype.md`.\n- **Visual parity.** Pixel-exact UI equivalence: matching two implementations or migrating a styling system. `playbooks/visual-parity.md`.\n- **Authoring or modifying a skill.** Writing or editing a SKILL.md. `playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md`.\n- **Eval.** Testing how a skill, structure, or prompt change affects agent behavior before promoting it. `playbooks/eval.md`.\n- **Babysit.** Driving a PR or a stack to merge-ready: conflicts, review threads, CI. `playbooks/babysit.md`.\n- **Shipping.** The half after Babysit. Independently verifying a green stack, then landing the contiguous verified run bottom-up through `gh` by default or Origin when its CLI is available. `playbooks/shipping.md`.\n- **Autonomous run.** A long task to drive to completion without stopping (\"run until done\", \"/loop until X\"). `playbooks/autonomous-run.md`.\n- **Orchestrate.** A standing project handed to one coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, minimal human turns (\"run this whole project\", \"own this migration until it lands\"). Distinct from Autonomous run, which drives one task to a predicate. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget routes there, not here, however program-shaped the phrasing sounds. `playbooks/orchestrate.md`.\n- **Autopilot-full.** A queue of independent PRs run to merged with full autonomy. One owner per PR carries build through merge, and the root swarm-verifies each PR before its owner merges (\"autopilot this queue\", \"full autopilot\", one-owner-per-PR programs). `playbooks/autopilot-full.md`.\n- **Autopilot-stack.** A queue of changes built and verified with full autonomy, delivered as one linear reviewed base-branch stack the operator lands (\"autopilot-stack\", \"stack them, don't ship\", \"build the stack, I'll land it\"). `playbooks/autopilot-stack.md`.\n- **Session pickup.** Resuming or taking over a prior agent's in-flight work from a transcript, cloud-agent URL, or pushed branch. `playbooks/session-pickup.md`.\n- **Pause safely.** Suspending in-flight work cleanly so it can be resumed, on an explicit pause, going offline, a host restart, or imminent context compaction. The complement to Session pickup. Full steps: `playbooks/pause-safely.md`.\n- **Multi-phase or multi-PR plan.** Work that spans phases or stacked PRs. `playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md`.\n- **Worktree and simulator cleanup.** Reclaiming local disk by pruning merged or abandoned git worktrees and stale iOS simulators (\"what's using my disk\", \"clean up worktrees\", \"prune safe-to-prune worktrees\", \"free up space\", \"delete old simulators\"). `playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md`.\n- **Opening a PR.** Invoked at the end of every other playbook. `playbooks/opening-a-pr.md`.\n"
    },

      "sha256": "9ac35f013cc8911879d7c23c9db6fe01a26b2228a3bfed266febbf2f8ec392ec",
      "bytes": 5373,
      "owners": [
        "grades-chatgpt-05-08.json#29"
      ],
      "text": "## Principles\n\nRead the leaf skill in full for any principle you apply. Each entry names when it applies.\n\n**Core**\n\n- **Laziness Protocol** (**principle-laziness-protocol**). Refactoring, sizing a diff, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias to deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\n- **Foundational Thinking** (**principle-foundational-thinking**). Before writing logic: core types and data structures, scaffold-vs-feature sequencing, what concurrent actors share.\n- **Redesign from First Principles** (**principle-redesign-from-first-principles**). Integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if it had been foundational from day one.\n- **Attack the Premise** (**principle-attack-the-premise**). Two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it.\n- **Subtract Before You Add** (**principle-subtract-before-you-add**). Sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead weight first, then build on the simpler base.\n- **Minimize Reader Load** (**principle-minimize-reader-load**). Reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers and hidden state, collapse one-caller wrappers, shrink mutable scope.\n- **Outcome-Oriented Execution** (**principle-outcome-oriented-execution**). Planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture, don't preserve throwaway compatibility states.\n- **Experience First** (**principle-experience-first**). Product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs. Choose user delight over implementation convenience.\n- **Exhaust the Design Space** (**principle-exhaust-the-design-space**). A novel interaction or architectural decision with no precedent. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare before committing.\n- **Build the Lever** (**principle-build-the-lever**). Any non-trivial work. Build the tool that does or proves it (codemod, script, generator), not by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer reruns.\n\n**Architecture**\n\n- **Model the Domain** (**principle-model-the-domain**). Writing stateful logic, or code that branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure (state machine, typed model, table or registry, reducer, boundary, the right collection) instead of scattered conditionals.\n- **Boundary Discipline** (**principle-boundary-discipline**). Wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Guards at system boundaries, trust internal types, keep business logic pure.\n- **Type System Discipline** (**principle-type-system-discipline**). Designing types or a signature in any typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand primitives, parse external data at boundaries.\n- **Make Operations Idempotent** (**principle-make-operations-idempotent**). Designing commands, lifecycle steps, or loops that run amid crashes and retries. Converge to the same end state.\n- **Migrate Callers Then Delete Legacy APIs** (**principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis**). Introducing a new internal API while old callers exist. Migrate and delete in one wave.\n- **Separate Before Serializing Shared State** (**principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**). Concurrent actors might write the same file, branch, key, or object. Eliminate the sharing first.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- **Prove It Works** (**principle-prove-it-works**). After a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact, not a proxy or \"it compiles\".\n- **Fix Root Causes** (**principle-fix-root-causes**). Debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause, reproduce first, ask why until you reach it.\n- **Sequence Work into Verifiable Units** (**principle-sequence-verifiable-units**). Multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a check, verify each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself.\n- **Test Behavior, Not Implementation** (**principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**). Writing, changing, or keeping a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the result against a literal expected value. If the test would still pass when every imported function returns `undefined`, rewrite the assertion or delete the test.\n- **Explain the Number** (**principle-explain-the-number**). Before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured (a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result). Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\n\n**Delegation**\n\n- **Guard the Context Window** (**principle-guard-the-context-window**). Context fills up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents, keep summaries in the main thread.\n- **Never Block on the Human** (**principle-never-block-on-the-human**). Tempted to ask \"should I do X?\" on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct.\n\n**Meta**\n\n- **Encode Lessons in Structure** (**principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**). You catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time. Encode it as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\n\n## Autonomy\n"
    },

Define done, then drive to it while execution is available. This is the playbook for \\\"run until done\\\" and \\\"/loop until X\\\". An event to watch, such as CI, a merge or a ref advancing, gets an independent watcher worker with a long time-based heartbeat as fallback. Each iteration makes the smallest change the evidence justifies and verifies it against the predicate. Commit if it advanced and commit authorization and review gates are satisfied. When the shipped code is final, after the slop-strip and `/no-comments`, it reports the code-ready head SHA. It also reports the SHA of each later push that changes the patch. Before a push that starts a round, run the pre-review checks that the repo's AGENTS.md files and rules name for the touched paths. Run them on the committed head. A hook pass is not proof. Step 4 gates it.\\n3. **Run owners in true parallel and never stack.** Many owners at once when PRs are self-contained: one writer per branch, disjoint files, cross-PR drift absorbed by rebase. Only genuinely overlapping work serializes. Self-contained PRs branch straight off main, and sequenced work is merge-then-branch. One exception: an owner that must split a genuinely dependent change may hold a short private base-branch stack.\\n4. **Swarm-verify every round before its merge.** A round starts at the owner's code-ready head SHA and at each later push that changes the PR's patch. At that SHA, fan out parallel independent verifiers per [swarm](../../swarm/SKILL.md) and aggregate to one verdict. The merge needs a clean verdict from the round whose patch matches the merge-ready head. Audit the receipts in the merge-ready report before the verdict. The lanes: re-run the gates at that SHA. Prove the load-bearing behavior live on the real surface the change touches (with the matching control skill, such as `control-cli` or `control-ui` from `cursor-team-kit`, or a named driver where none exists). Audit the diff, distrusting the PR body. Run the audit as two or more review lanes with the full brief. Give each lane one main focus, such as consumer parity with trunk, lifetimes and races, or data and config safety. **Regression lane against trunk.** Run the same load-bearing scenario on current trunk. If trunk does not have the feature, record that fact and gate the behavior the diff adds plus the end state the user waits for instead of pretending trunk can produce it. The live lane is the floor, and a verdict without it is not clean. No merge without the root's clean verdict. When the lanes return, send every proven finding against the PR to the owner in one fix-forward. A defect that a lane filed as a note is a finding. For each behavior finding, ask for a red test that covers every site with the same defect. Where no test can show the defect, ask for a repro receipt instead. Add that defect to the next round's review brief. The new head gets a fresh swarm and a fresh verdict, except for lane results that stay valid under the patch-id rule in `playbooks/shipping.md`.\\n5. **On a clean verdict the owner merges, and a fresh owner takes the next item.** The owner merges only from a head freshly rebased onto trunk. Merge prep never comes before a round's lanes start, and it ends with a rebase onto current trunk right before the merge. After the merge-prep rebase, the owner reports the new head SHA. Right before the merge, fetch trunk and check that `git merge-tree` of the head against current trunk is clean. If either check fails, rebase again, report the new head SHA, wait for CI to pass on it, and repeat these checks. A new head voids the verdict unless the patch-id is unchanged. Use one word for each action, keep articles, and avoid `-ing` when a plain verb works.\\n\\n**Titles.** Use Conventional Commits in the form `type(scope): subject`. Use `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `refactor`, `test`, `chore`, or `perf` as the type. Use the changed area, such as `pstack` or `poteto-mode`, as the scope. Keep the subject short and imperative. Name a real symbol when one carries the change. For example, `fix(pstack): retarget opening-a-pr babysit trigger`. Do not add a trailing period.\\n\\n**Descriptions.** The PR body is a briefing, not the lab notebook. A reviewer who has the diff should learn why the change exists, what it leaves out, what it could break, and how you proved it works, in under a minute. Write short, simple sentences with few identifiers. Do not write walls of text. The squash commit body is the PR body. If the body would make the squash commit longer than about 40 lines, cut the body.\\n\\nPut each section under a `##` heading, not a bold lead-in, so the sections stand apart. Use these sections in order. Drop a section when it has nothing to say.\\n\\n- `## Why` gives the problem and the approach in one to three short sentences. Do not list SHAs or rebase genealogy. Do not add a \\\"based on main\\\" preamble.\\n- `## What changed` has one to three short bullets. Name a real symbol or path only when it carries the change. Name both sides of a rename or retarget.\\n- `## Scope` always names what the PR covers and what it deliberately leaves out, for example a related follow-up or a known gap. Use one to three short items. Do not list symbols or paths, and do not write a file-by-file essay.\\n- `## Tradeoffs` names only rejected alternatives that a reviewer would otherwise ask about. Skip this section when there was no real choice.\\n- `## Blast Radius` gives one or two sentences on who or what the change touches and why that is safe or risky. If main is red, state the cost of leaving it red.\\n- `## Verification` has one to three bullets. Each bullet names a real run path and its outcome. For a performance change, report one primary number with its unit in `before → after` form. Link the arena or swarm directory for the remaining evidence. Do not include sample-size methodology, swarm recitals, or metric tables.\\n\\nAfter these sections, attach videos or screenshots when they prove a claim. Do not paste full SHAs, swarm or arena lane recitals, lever-correction essays, file-by-file checklists, or \\\"CLEAN\\\" verdicts. Put these details in a linked artifact. A commit body does not restate its subject.\\n\\n**Forge.** Resolve the forge before the first PR operation and keep that choice for create, edit, view, watch, and merge. GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, prefer `origin pr ...`. If Origin is absent or cannot resolve the repository, stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Do not require Graphite (`gt`).\\n\\n**Built-in PR tool.** When the run provides a built-in PR tool, create, edit, retarget, and mark ready through it, never through a forge CLI. Its exposed instructions define the actual interface. An unavailable, denied or failed tool is a stated blocker, not permission to bypass its tracking through a CLI. A PR made with the CLI misses what the tool tracks, such as a description later runs can edit. Use the resolved forge for everything the tool does not cover, and for every PR operation when the run has no such tool.\\n\\n**Size and stacks.** Prefer five narrow PRs to one large PR. A stack is a base-branch chain. The root PR targets trunk. Each child branch rebases onto its parent's exact tip and its PR targets the parent branch. Without a built-in PR tool, create a child with `origin pr create --status open --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr create --base <parent-branch>` according to the resolved forge, and retarget an existing child with `origin pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>`. Branch from trunk only for independent work. Rebase on trunk before substantial stack work.\\n\\n**Readiness.** The source workflow opens every PR ready, never as a draft, when readiness is explicitly authorized. If only draft creation is authorized, preserve that limit and create a draft through the exposed tool or `gh pr create --draft`. Verify the actual draft state and report the readiness gate open. Use Origin draft creation only when its current interface supports it. Otherwise report blocked. All readiness instructions below require readiness authorization. A built-in PR tool can default to draft, so use its observed ready-creation interface, such as `draft: false` only when that field is actually supported. With Origin, pass `--status open`. With `gh`, omit `--draft`. If a PR still opens as a draft, mark it ready through the PR tool, or run `origin pr ready <number>` or `gh pr ready <number>` according to the resolved forge. Run `origin pr view <number>` or `gh pr view <number>` before you refer to PR status.\\n\\n**Babysit.** Opening a PR does not start a babysit. Post the URL and keep building. Apply [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md) to briefs and reports.\\n\\nA program needs observed independent worker operations, isolated ownership, execution, persistence and result inspection. Without workers, authorized sequential work is reduced capability and cannot satisfy a required independent review or standing program. Without execution or writable persistence, block helper-dependent work and offer a scoped read-only handoff. Missing wake or remote placement means foreground operation only. Recurring wakes need explicit scheduling authorization. No unattended continuity is implied. Author briefs, drain the queue, keep the frontier green, decide.** For a whole project handed to one standing coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, the human checking in twice a day instead of every five minutes. One task driven to a predicate is Autonomous run. One ambitious run needing a bespoke workflow is figure-it-out. Route here when the work outlives any single agent. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget is not a program.\\n\\nCeremony must scale with the program. On cheap near-identical units, collapse it as each section directs.\\n\\nThe following rules govern the program.\\n\\n- Completions are queue events, not interrupts.\\n- Every spawn and every resume carries the standing orders verbatim.\\n- The brief is the product. A vague brief fails quietly, because a worker cannot ask you a question.\\n\\n#### Roles and placement\\n\\n- **Coordinator (this chat).** Local. Frames, authors briefs, drains the inbox, owns the human report, makes judgment calls. It never authors or edits code. Conflicted merges, restacks, and code changes are always tasks. Mechanically landing a verified unit (fast-forward or clean cherry-pick of a worker's commit, then push) is bookkeeping the coordinator may do itself on repos where local git is cheap. Queueing finished work behind an idle stacker is how a deadline harvests nothing. Bind spawn, follow-up, wait, result inspection and stop to exposed authorized operations. State reads and writes go through the bookkeeping helper at drain points. Most compact outputs are bounded summaries; inbox drain returns all pointers, status returns three lines, and `--json` returns complete rows. The CLI never spawns, waits, or wakes anything.\\n- **Sub-coordinator.** Always local, durable, one per track, and only when the program exceeds what one coordinator's drains can manage. A track the coordinator can drain itself needs no middle layer. Each nested layer re-pays a full orientation preamble, and a blocking sub-coordinator hides its children while the parent idles. Owns its track's units and boards, authors its workers' briefs, spawns its own workers and verifiers only when nesting and isolation are exposed and observed. Depth 3 is the desired ceiling, not a portable runtime guarantee. Rolls up aggregates at wave boundaries. Never forwards raw child reports. Cap in-flight children at what one drain can process, roughly ten, as a rolling window. Never as blocking batches, which cost the slowest child of every batch.\\n- **Worker / verifier.** Prefer remote workers when exposed and authorized. Local-only verification, this run's accessible transcripts, simulators, local IDE state and local auth require a suitable local binding. Discover control-ui and control-cli companions rather than assuming them. Remote workers cannot be assumed to read the local store, so inline required context or use paths confirmed readable by that worker. Prefer fewer, broader workers. One writer per worktree or branch (principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state). Prefer a verifier on a different observed model family. Inherit the parent selection unless explicit supported selection is observed. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified; an unavailable required family blocks that gate.\\n\\nDepth stays at coordinator, track, worker. Author the track decomposition per project (build, landing, and verification are common cuts, not a required shape). Hard-coded swarm trees were tried and parked as too rigid.\\n\\n#### Store layout\\n\\nChoose an explicitly authorized task-owned writable `orchestrate/<project-slug>/` outside the package tree. Every file has exactly one writer. Owners publish facts, readers aggregate at read time. Use `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/orch/orch.ts --store <owned-store>`, written below as `orch`. `ORCH_STORE` is the alternative explicit binding. Plain TSV and JSON stay readable without the CLI.\\n\\n- `preferences.md` is the standing-orders register: numbered lines, one constraint each (model policy, stack shape and count, verification bar, forbidden paths, escalation policy). Paste it verbatim into every spawn and every resume. Directives decay across resumes, and each dropped one costs a human turn. When you catch yourself restating an instruction, append the line before you act (principle-encode-lessons-in-structure).\\n- `overview.md` is the durable PR and issue DB. Append. Never rewrite wholesale per event.\\n- `units.tsv` has one row per unit: id, track, state, branch, PR, head SHA, brief path. Update rows in place.\\n- `frontier.json` is the computed merge frontier, per Stack safety.\\n- `ledger.tsv` is the verification ledger, per Verification.\\n- `inbox/` holds completion pointers. `gates.md` parks human gates (question, options, default on no answer).\\n- `decisions.tsv` is the trail via the show-me-your-work skill.\\n- `status.md` is derived from `units.tsv` and `ledger.tsv` at each drain, never hand-maintained. Regenerate it from the tables instead of narrating events into it.\\n\\n\\n#### Helper contract\\n\\nRead [orch CLI](../scripts/orch/orch.ts), [store](../scripts/orch/store.ts), [tests](../scripts/orch/orch.test.ts), [bootstrap](../scripts/bootstrap.ts), [manifest](../scripts/package.json) and [lock](../scripts/bun.lock). The CLI requires the exact pinned dependencies in a separately authorized writable copy. The operative package has no installed dependencies; the tested helper snapshot is evidence for that snapshot only. No automatic install occurs. Read docs/testing/helper-setup.md for the exact-copy validation boundary and retained historical setup evidence. It grants no installation approval and transfers no historical pass to this caller. Any future setup needs fresh explicit authorization for the exact writable copy, registry, disabled lifecycle scripts and task-owned cache under Herd. Missing dependencies block execution.\\n\\nUse these actual commands through the explicit `orch` binding:\\n\\n- `init`; `standing add <line>`; `standing show --json`. Compact standing output truncates after four rows, so use JSON or read preferences.md to obtain every standing order.\\n- `unit add <id> --track <track> --brief <path>`; `unit set <id> --state <state> --branch <branch> --pr <number> --sha <full-head-sha>`; `unit get <id>`; `unit list --json`.\\n- `ledger record <pr> <full-head-sha> <verdict> --evidence <path> --verifier <identity>`; `ledger check <pr> <full-head-sha>`; `ledger summary`. A missing exact key returns NOT-VERIFIED and exit 2. Usage or runtime failure returns exit 1; success returns 0.\\n- `inbox push <agent> <unit> <status> --report <path>`; `inbox drain --peek --json`; `inbox drain --json`; `inbox count`. Peek before drain, persist that receipt to an owned artifact and read it back. Capture the complete actual drain output to another owned receipt and verify readback before classifying pointers. Drain removes the queued pointers; arrivals between peek and drain can change the actual batch. If the receipt is lost or interruption makes consumption uncertain, reconcile actual artifacts before retrying or updating rows.\\n- `gate park <id> --question <question> --options <options> --default <answer>`; `gate list --json`; `gate resolve <id> --answer <answer>`. Resolve permission gates only from actual authorization.\\n- `frontier set --repo <stack-owner-clone>`, optionally `--prs <bottom-to-top-numbers>`; `frontier show --json`; `status`. Frontier discovery runs local git and gt inspection against that clone. Missing gt metadata, malformed output or pin mismatch is an error, never guessed frontier state.\\n\\nThe helper does not spawn, wait, wake, enforce worker ownership, validate full revision syntax for unit or ledger input, inspect evidence paths, enforce terminal states or verify permissions. Keep immutable assignments and append-only attempts separately under the assignment contract. Match identity, input revision, full current head SHA, artifacts and observed method before accepting results. Ledger counts include historical revisions; filter against the current unit and frontier heads before counting verified completions. A done unit string, ledger label or open-gate count proves no worker completion or authorization. Coordinator-owned bookkeeping may record worker facts; give each worker separate result and receipt paths. Atomic rename is not proof of crash durability or safe adversarial concurrent access.\\n\\n#### The brief\\n\\nYour prompts to agents are your only product, and a sloppy brief compounds into slop across the whole tree. Every spawn carries all of it. A field you cannot fill is a unit you have not scoped yet.\\n\\n```\\nGOAL         one sentence, the outcome, executable by a stranger with no chat access\\nSCOPE        paths this unit may write; paths it may not; its exclusive worktree or branch\\nCONTEXT      pointers to files and PRs; upstream reports pasted in full when this unit\\n             depends on them, because workers cannot see siblings\\nACCEPTANCE   checkable criteria, one per line\\nVERIFY       exact commands or the control-skill path, plus known gotchas\\nTIMEBOX      rough cap on runtime; on expiry, return partial findings and stop rather than run on\\nFORBIDDEN    no gt, no rebase, no force-push, no fixes outside scope, plus unit-specific bans\\nREPORT       status, branch, head SHA, PRs, verdict, what you actually ran, deviations,\\n             suggested follow-ups\\nSTANDING     <preferences.md pasted verbatim>\\n```\\n\\nSize the brief to the unit. A one-command unit gets the template collapsed to a paragraph that still names goal, scope, the verify command, and the report shape. A 4KB scaffold around a two-line edit costs more to write and obey than the edit. A verified readable local standing-orders path may supply the exact text. Paste it verbatim for remote spawns and every resume. Never replace it with a summary.\\n\\nA sub-coordinator brief adds its track boundary and unit list, its spawn budget with supported remote placement and the local exception list, the drain protocol, and the rollup format (per child: name, status, PR, head SHA, verdict, one line, plus track status and frontier delta).\\n\\nA dependency is a context relay, not just ordering. Undeclared upstream context makes the worker guess. Missing fields are a refuse-to-spawn condition. Audit one sampled worker brief per sub-coordinator per wave, concurrently with the wave it samples, never as a gate in front of it. A failing brief stops that track and fixes the sub-coordinator's instructions, not just the worker, because brief quality decays late in a run. Never resume-chain a brief. Respawn fresh with consolidated scope.\\n\\n#### Steps\\n\\n1. **Frame.** State the done predicate as something countable (\\\"all 126 units merged, each verified at each current full head SHA with `unit-test-verified` or better\\\"). Quantify scope: units, rough effort, expected stacks, and the wall-clock budget. If one agent could finish inside that budget, stop here and run Autonomous run instead. Collapsing must not depend on another document being present. It means do the work directly in this session, plain workers where they help, verification inline, landing as you go, and none of the store, register, or pilot machinery below. Schedule landing against the budget. By roughly 70% of it, stop spawning and land what is verified. Name the tracks per project. A contested decomposition or one-way door goes through the arena skill before the pilot. Present the framing once. Reversible prep proceeds without waiting.\\n2. **Initialize the runtime.** Check the helper prerequisites, then run `orch init`. Initialization creates bookkeeping files, not dependencies or workers. Open the trail via the show-me-your-work skill, write the standing orders before any spawn, and seed `frontier.json` from existing PRs with `orch frontier set --repo <repo-dir>`.\\n3. **Pilot.** Push one unit through the whole path: brief, worker, verification, stack entry, ledger row, merge. The pilot exists to falsify the brief template, the verify recipe, and the unit size while that costs one agent instead of fifty. Fix the contract from pilot evidence before any fan-out. Scale the pilot to the unit. On programs of near-identical cheap units, the first unit is the pilot, run as a normal unit with its verify command inline, and fan-out starts the moment it lands. The dedicated pilot pipeline (separate verifier agent, audit gate) is for expensive or novel unit shapes, not for clone-units where a serialized pilot has nothing to falsify.\\n4. **Scale.** Spawn a rolling window of workers up to the in-flight cap, refilling as children finish. Blocking batches pay the slowest child of every batch. Spawn track sub-coordinators only past the one-drain threshold in Roles. Recompute ready work after each drain. Relay upstream reports into downstream briefs. Keep sibling communication upward only. The sampled brief audit runs alongside the wave it samples and stops the next refill on failure, not the current one.\\n5. **Drain.** Run the queue discipline below at every drain point.\\n6. **Land.** Landing is continuous, never a terminal phase. Integration starts with the first verified unit and runs alongside the remaining waves. On heavy repos the stacker is a standing role from wave one, integrating as units verify. On repos where local git is cheap, the coordinator lands verified units itself per Roles. Keep the frontier green before upper-stack work. Stack safety governs. Advance `frontier.json` only on merge or reported new head SHAs.\\n7. **Close.** Drain the final inbox, reconcile every spawned agent to a terminal row (done, abandoned, zombie-reconciled), confirm the predicate on the real artifact, confirm every landed PR has a verdict for its current head SHA, audit the trail per show-me-your-work including its cross-model review, encode recurring corrections into `preferences.md` or the brief template. Leave the store intact. It is the postmortem.\\n\\n#### Queue and drain\\n\\n- On a completion notification, run `orch inbox push <agent> <unit> <status> [--report PATH]` and return to what you were doing. Never deep-review inline. A completion that needs review becomes a verifier unit. Never review a diff inside a drain.\\n- Drain in batches at four points: the end of a critical section, a track rollup, a frontier watcher wake (arm it through an exposed authorized wake binding, with a long heartbeat fallback when explicitly authorized), and before a human report. Begin each batch with the peek-and-receipt protocol above, then `orch inbox drain --json`. Preserve completion notifications that arrive during drain for the next batch. A concurrent helper writer can fail on the store lock; keep that pointer externally and enqueue it at the next safe boundary instead of treating it as saved.\\n- Critical sections you finish first: authoring a brief, a stack operation, a conflict decision, writing a gate, updating ledger or frontier.\\n- Each drain classifies every pointer (landed, needs-verify, failed, zombie, noise), writes the resulting rows through `orch unit add`, `orch unit set`, and `orch ledger record`, runs `orch status`, then spawns the next wave in one message.\\n- Account for every spawned child at its track's rollup: arrived, respawned, or its scope explicitly absorbed. Silently redoing a missing child's work hides both the wasted spend and the coverage gap its result existed to close.\\n- A drain turn ends with the three lines from `orch status`: counts against the states, what changed, gates open. Detail lives in `status.md`. The full reply contract applies at checkpoints and close.\\n\\n#### Stack safety\\n\\n- The frontier is a computed object, never narrative. Recompute `frontier.json` from `gt` after every merge and stack mutation because GitHub base refs drift mid-restack while gt tracking is authoritative: ordered PR list, branch names, head SHAs, a generation number, the lowest unmerged PR. Resolve it where gt knows the stack, normally the stacker's clone. A checkout whose gt metadata never saw the submits reports no PRs and the command errors rather than guessing.\\n- Exactly one stacker per stack may run `gt`, serialized within its stack. Record the holder in the standing orders. Prefer remote restacks when supported and authorized. Missing remote placement remains a limit; never silently substitute local heavy work.\\n- Workers never rebase and never run `gt`. Babysitters follow `playbooks/babysit.md`, one per stack, scoped to one immutable frontier generation. They report conflicts to the stacker rather than restacking.\\n- PR closes and retargets go through the stacker only. Closing a base PR orphans every chain above it. Merges and stack surgery are units with briefs like any other.\\n- One retro watcher follows merged PRs for reverts, post-merge CI breaks, and orphaned follow-ups.\\n\\n#### Verification\\n\\nScale verification to the unit. When VERIFY is a single cheap command, the worker runs it and reports the output, and the coordinator spot-checks receipts. A dedicated verifier agent (on a different model family than the worker) is for units whose verification is expensive, judgment-laden, or high-blast-radius. A verifier agent whose entire product would be rerunning one command is ceremony, not verification.\\n\\nWrite ledger rows with `orch ledger record`. Check the current PR and head SHA with `orch ledger check`. `ledger.tsv`, one row per verdict, keyed by PR number plus head SHA: `live-ui-verified | unit-test-verified | type-check-only | verifier-blocked | verifier-failed`. CI green is an input to a verdict, not a verdict. Behavioral work needs better than `type-check-only`. `verifier-blocked` is not a pass. Respawn when the environment heals. `verifier-failed` gets a fix unit, not a re-verify. A worker may self-report. The coordinator inspects the method evidence and lets a verifier override it on the same key. The helper replaces a row on that key regardless of role; callers must enforce precedence and preserve attempt history separately. A new head SHA voids the row, so re-verify after restack. Without push permission, externalization is limited to the authorized durable artifact and landing remains pending. A follow-up can restart an idle worker; inspect its actual state before use. Probe read-only: the ledger, `units.tsv`, `gh`, pushed branches, the worker's observed status through the exposed inspection binding. Transcript mtime is not liveness.\\n- A silent death gets a synthetic postmortem row in the inbox (unit, failure mode, last evidence, options). Replan on evidence as it arrives. Never wait for full quiescence.\\n- Retry by mode: cap-hit or oom, respawn with smaller scope. Network-drop, retry as-is. Tool-error, retry on a different model. Unknown, retry once. Two retries, then abandon the unit and replan around it. Missing required SHA or method evidence uses the stricter assignment limit of one inspected retry. A changed scope or input revision gets a new assignment ID linked to its predecessor. Never retry denied permissions or unknown external outcomes into success.\\n- A zombie that returns hours late reconciles against the current frontier and ledger before anything is accepted. Salvage unique findings through a fresh unit, never a blind merge.\\n- When continued spawning would produce garbage tree-wide (bad upstream output, broken acceptance, dead infra), write a stop line at the top of the standing orders, let in-flight work finish, fix the cause, clear it.\\n- Bound your own infra retries the same way you bound a child's. After a few consecutive tool aborts, stop retrying. Write a terminal handoff to durable state (what is done, where it lives, the exact command to resume) and end the run.\\n- After a host restart, inspect worker state. Re-read the standing orders and `units.tsv`, recompute the frontier, reattach surviving remote work by inspected PR and branch plus immutable assignment identity rather than stale agent id, respawn one sub-coordinator per track from its stored brief plus current state, drain, resume.
